I mean, I probably do small things without fully thinking them through sometimes, like anyone, but I wouldn’t say I’m especially impulsive or doing risky stuff without considering it. At least, nothing really comes to mind. Does that seem like what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself fidgeting or feeling restless when you're not at work, like when you're at home or out with friends?\n\nPatient: A little, yeah… I mean, I notice it more in settings where I’m supposed to be sitting still, like trainings or longer team meetings. I’ll be bouncing my leg or clicking a pen or shifting around in the chair, even when I’m trying to look focused. I don’t know if it’s that noticeable to other people, but I do catch myself doing it.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Hi, um… thanks for seeing me. I guess the main reason I came in is work-related. I process insurance claims, and I’ve been having this problem where I keep getting up from my workstation during the day, even when I know I’m supposed to stay there and finish my claim reviews. It’s not like I’m trying to avoid work, exactly… I just keep finding myself up and away from my desk, and it’s starting to feel like something I should get help with, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious or worried when you're sitting at your desk working on claims?\n\nPatient: Um… I don’t think so, not really. I mean, I wouldn’t describe it as anxiety exactly. I’m not sitting there feeling panicky or worried about the claims, at least not that I’ve noticed. It’s more like… I just end up not staying put. But I don’t know if I’m explaining that the right way.\n\nDoctor: Do you have any recurring thoughts that you can't stop thinking about?\n\nPatient: Um… recurring thoughts? I don’t think so. Not in the sense of something looping in my head that I can’t stop thinking about. I mean, I can get stuck on normal work stuff sometimes, but I wouldn’t say there’s one thought that keeps coming back or that feels out of my control. Does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have to get up from your desk for some reason, like something is compelling you to do so?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say it feels like I’m compelled exactly… that sounds a little stronger than how it feels to me. But yeah, the main thing is I keep getting up from my workstation during the day, even when I know I’m supposed to stay there and finish my claim reviews. It’s like I just don’t stay put the way I should.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel restless in your body when you're sitting at your desk?\n\nPatient: Yeah… maybe a little. I don’t know if I’d call it “restless” exactly, but in longer meetings or trainings I do notice I’m kind of bouncing my leg or clicking a pen, shifting around in the chair. I’m usually trying to still look focused, so I don’t think it’s super obvious, but… yeah, I guess my body doesn’t totally stay still.\n\nDoctor: Do you often act on impulse without thinking things through?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, not in a big way. At least, nothing really comes to mind. Does that seem like what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself fidgeting or feeling restless when you're not at work, like when you're at home or out with friends?\n\nPatient: A little, yeah… I mean, I notice it more in settings where I’m supposed to be sitting still, like trainings or longer team meetings. I process insurance claims, and I’ve been having this problem where I keep getting up from my workstation during the day, even when I know I’m supposed to stay there and finish my claim reviews. It’s not like I’m trying to avoid work, exactly… I just keep finding myself up and away from my desk, and it’s starting to feel like something I should get help with, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious or worried when you're sitting at your desk working on claims?\n\nPatient: Um… I don’t think so, not really. I mean, I wouldn’t describe it as anxiety exactly. I’m not sitting there feeling panicky or worried about the claims, at least not that I’ve noticed. It’s more like… I just end up not staying put. But I don’t know if I’m explaining that the right way.\n\nDoctor: Do you have any recurring thoughts that you can't stop thinking about?\n\nPatient: Um… recurring thoughts? I don’t think so. Not in the sense of something looping in my head that I can’t stop thinking about. I mean, I can get stuck on normal work stuff sometimes, but I wouldn’t say there’s one thought that keeps coming back or that feels out of my control. Does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have to get up from your desk for some reason, like something is compelling you to do so?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say it feels like I’m compelled exactly… that sounds a little stronger than how it feels to me. But yeah, the main thing is I keep getting up from my workstation during the day, even when I know I’m supposed to stay there and finish my claim reviews. It’s like I just don’t stay put the way I should.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel restless in your body when you're sitting at your desk?\n\nPatient: Yeah… maybe a little. I don’t know if I’d call it “restless” exactly, but in longer meetings or trainings I do notice I’m kind of bouncing my leg or clicking a pen, shifting around in the chair. I’m usually trying to still look focused, so I don’t think it’s super obvious, but… yeah, I guess my body doesn’t totally stay still.\n\nDoctor: Do you often act on impulse without thinking things through?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, not in a big way. At least, nothing really comes to mind. Does that seem like what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself fidgeting or feeling restless when you're not at work, like when you're at home or out with friends?\n\nPatient: A little, yeah… I mean, I notice it more in settings where I’m supposed to be sitting still, like trainings or longer team meetings. I’ll be bouncing my leg or clicking a pen or shifting around in the chair, even when I’m trying to look focused. I don’t know if it’s that noticeable to other people, but I do catch myself doing it.\n\nDoctor: Do you have thoughts that keep repeating in your mind, even when you try to focus on something else?\n\nPatient: Hmm… I’m not sure. Then I realize I didn’t actually take in what I just read, so I have to reread the same section a few times. I try to catch it, but yeah, it does happen.\n\nDoctor: Do you experience these feelings of restlessness and difficulty staying focused at night, or is it mainly during the day?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure I’ve noticed a pattern at night, honestly. Does that answer what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Do you experience restlessness when you're alone at home?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure, honestly. I wouldn’t say it’s a major thing I’ve noticed… maybe just normal fidgeting sometimes? I’m trying to look like I’m paying attention, so I don’t think it’s super obvious, but it does happen in those kinds of situations too. If I’m reading through a claim and people nearby start talking about another case, or I suddenly remember some random errand I need to do, my attention kind of slides off. I try to catch it, but yeah, it does happen.\n\nDoctor: Do you experience these feelings of restlessness and difficulty staying focused at night, or is it mainly during the day?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure I’ve noticed a pattern at night, honestly. I think I notice it more during the day, like when I’m working or in meetings. At night I’m usually not tracking it as much… I might just be tired or trying to unwind, so it’s harder for me to say. Does that answer what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Do you experience restlessness when you're alone at home?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure, honestly. I’m trying to look like I’m paying attention, so I don’t think it’s super obvious, but it does happen in those kinds of situations too. Does that count as restlessness?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel restless when you're in a quiet, comfortable environment, like your own home?\n\nPatient: At home when it’s quiet… I don’t know, I’m not sure I’d call it restless exactly. I might shift around or get up if I feel like it, but since I’m comfortable and nobody’s expecting me to sit still, I don’t notice it as much. So I guess I haven’t really paid attention to it in that setting. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you think your restlessness is usually triggered by feeling anxious or overwhelmed?\n\nPatient: I don’t think it’s always anxiety, no. Does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Has your restlessness affected your personal relationships or daily activities outside of work?\n\nPatient: Um… outside of work, I’m not really sure.
